### Page 1

10月 LPR报价维持不变；资金面宽松充裕，债市延续强势 
【内容摘要】10月20日，资金面宽松充裕；债市延续强势；转债市场主要指数集体收涨，转
债个券多数上涨；各期限美债收益率走势分化，主要欧洲经济体10年期国债收益率普遍下行。 
一、债市要闻 
（一）国内要闻 
【10月 LPR报价出炉：5年期和1年期利率均维持不变】10月20日，据央行官网发布，10月
贷款市场报价利率（LPR）报价出炉：5年期以上LPR为 3.5%，上月为3.5%。1年期LPR为3%，
上月为3%。 
【国家发展改革委：正研究制定扩大内需战略实施方案】国家发展改革委有关负责人在10月
20日举行的新闻发布会上表示，正在研究制定扩大内需战略实施方案（2026-2030年），并将
加快推动印发实施。此外，国家发展改革委会同有关部门推出首批136项标志性重大应用场景
项目，为场景培育和开放提供重要牵引，将建立高效联动的工作机制，加强技术、数据、人才、
资本等要素支撑保障，支持场景建设牵头单位健全场景需求发布、技术对接、成果展示、要素
匹配等具体办法。 
【前三季度工业对经济增长的贡献率保持在35%左右】10月20日，国务院新闻办公室举行新
闻发布会，介绍2026年前三季度工业和信息化发展情况。工业和信息化部总工程师王卫明介
绍，今年以来，工业和信息化高质量发展取得积极成效。一是经济运行稳中提质。坚持供需两
端协同发力，推动重点行业稳增长政策落地见效，扩大优质工业品供给，工业经济平稳运行，
生产、效益同步增长。前三季度，规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.3%，工业对经济增长的贡
献率保持在35%左右。前8个月，规模以上工业企业利润总额同比增长15.7%。数字产业加快
发展，收入同比增长14.6%。

（二）国际要闻

### Page 2

【美国9月成屋待售签约指数骤降7.3%，创2001年以来新低】10月20日，全美地产经纪商
协会（NAR）公布的数据显示，美国9月成屋待售合同签约指数骤降7.3%至66，创下2001年
有记录以来的最低值。同时，9月新屋开工量放缓至 2020年以来的最低水平之一，住房市场
的整体疲态进一步加剧。高企的抵押贷款利率是此次下滑的直接推手。抵押贷款利率自伊朗战
争爆发以来持续攀升，目前已升至近三年来的最高水平。NAR首席经济学家在声明中表示，“9
月市场状况因抵押贷款利率快速攀升而持续走弱，买卖双方可能都在等待利率出现有利转机
后再行动”。成屋待售合同签约数据历来被视为衡量成屋销售的前瞻性指标，因为合同签约通
常发生在成交完成前一至两个月。此次指数跌至历史低点，意味着未来数月内已完成交割的成
屋销售数据或将进一步承压。结合新屋开工放缓的数据，当前住房市场的困境在需求与供给两
端同步呈现，在高借贷成本与居高不下的房价双重压制下，市场的实质性改善仍取决于抵押贷
款利率的走向。
（三）大宗商品
【国际原油期货价格下跌，NYMEX天然气期货价格继续下跌】10月20日，WTI 11月原油期货

价格收跌 1.80%，报101.79美元/桶；布伦特12月原油期货价格收跌0.69%，报 108.79美元
/桶；COMEX 12月黄金期货价格收跌 0.42%，报4142.5美元/盎司；NYMEX天然气期货价格收
跌 1.15%，报2.993美元/百万英热单位。
二、资金面
（一）公开市场操作
10月 20日，央行公告称，根据公开市场业务一级交易商的需求，7天期逆回购操作量为零。
当日有650亿元逆回购到期，因此净回笼资金650亿元。
（二）资金利率
10月 20日，资金面宽松充裕，当日 DR001下行 0.18bp至 1.261%，DR007下行 0.17bp至
1.320%。

### Page 3

数据来源：Choice，东方金诚 
三、债市动态 
（一）利率债 
1.现券收益率走势 
10月 20日，央行宣布10月 21至 11月 2日持续开展隔夜逆回购，叠加昨日宣布政策利率换
锚，继续推升多头情绪，债市延续强势。截至北京时间20:00，10年期国债活跃券260016收
益率下行0.50bp至1.6250%，10年期国开债活跃券260205收益率下行0.50bp至1.6620%。

数据来源：Choice，东方金诚

### Page 4

2. 债券招标情况

数据来源：Choice，东方金诚 
（二）信用债 
1. 二级市场成交异动 
10月20日，1只产业债成交价格偏离幅度超10%，为“H0宝龙04”涨幅300%。 
2. 信用债事件 
◼ 荣盛发展：公司公告，拟对 38.516亿元债券进行重组，部分本金以物抵债、利息豁免。 
◼ 正荣地产：公司公告，针对香港附属公司的清盘呈请聆讯押后至11月9日。 
◼ 旭辉集团：公司公告，因违反财产报告制度，被萍乡市安源区人民法院列为失信被执行人，
执行标的39.725万元。 
◼ 东方时尚：公司公告，累计新增诉讼1件，涉及本金金额为15572.40元，占公司最近一
期经审计净资产的0.02%。 
◼ 紫金矿业：标普上调紫金矿业长期发行人信用评级至“BBB+”，展望“稳定”。 
◼ 牡国投：中诚信国际将牡丹江市国有资产投资控股有限公司（简称“牡国投”）主体长期
信用评级从“AA”上调至“AA+”，展望调整稳定。 
◼ 河南投资集团：惠誉确认河南投资集团“A”长期本外币发行人评级，展望“稳定”。 
◼ 中国银行迪拜分行：惠誉授予中国银行迪拜分行发行高级无抵押债券“A”最终评级。 
◼ 四川发展：惠誉确认四川发展“A”长期本外币发行人评级，展望“稳定”。

### Page 5

（三）可转债 
1. 权益及转债指数 
【权益市场三大股指涨跌不一】 10月20日，A股震荡盘整，机器人板块爆发，AI硬件标的
走势分化，红利板块有所调整，上证指数收涨 0.12%，深证成指、创业板指分别收跌 0.14%、
0.26%，全天成交额1.47万亿元。当日申万一级行业多数上涨，上涨行业中，计算机、机械设
备、综合、电子、医药生物涨超1%；下跌行业中，非银金融、煤炭跌逾1%。 
【转债市场主要指数集体收涨】 10月20日，转债市场延续上行，中证转债、上证转债、深
证转债分别收涨0.55%、0.52%、0.57%，转债市场成交额607.89亿元，较前一交易日缩量0.12
亿元。转债市场个券多数上涨，309只转债中，217只收涨，87只下跌，15只持平。当日上涨
个券中，新上市奥士转债涨停57.3%，存量个券中福新转债涨超13%；下跌个券中，灵康转债
跌逾2%，惠城转债等7支转债跌逾1%。

数据来源：Choice，东方金诚 
2. 转债跟踪 
◼ 今日（10月21日），航亚转债开启网上申购，满坤转债上市。

期美债收益率则下行3bp至5.12%。

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地
大幅上方修正で、9月
調査時点では過去5年
間で最高の増益計画

日本：企業の価格設定姿勢はなお強い。仕入価格再上昇にも警戒
5
 大企業・製造業の販売価格判断DIは前期並みの高水準を維持。企業は積極的な価格転嫁姿勢を継続
―6・7月の油価下落等を背景に素材業種の仕入価格判断DIが低下。対して販売価格判断DIは横ばい。コスト低下局面で
も販売価格を維持しており、企業利益の押し上げ要因に
 企業の中長期的な物価見通しも2％台半ばと、日銀の物価目標を上回る水準で高止まり
― 足元の原油価格再上昇やタンカー運賃高騰を受け、仕入価格が再び上昇する可能性には留意。企業の価格設定姿勢
が一段と強まるリスクも

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵

― 水平分業が確立した台湾電子産業が生産面で広く恩恵が波及
― メモリ主体・垂直統合モデルを特徴とする韓国半導体産業は、メモリ需給ひっ迫による価格高騰が経済を押し上げ

14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ

股價相對大盤走勢

欣銓 (3264 TT)  Buy

晶圓大廠新訂單逐步向上，建議 Buy。  
投資建議 ：欣銓 2026 年資本支出轉向積極擴張，主要來自與策
略客戶 (晶圓代工廠 )已確定之 ASIC 合作案，其龍潭新廠無塵室
與基礎設施建置，以及子公司全智科配合美系客戶擴產。 1Q26
至 3Q26 期間取得不動產、廠房及設備 (資本支出 )累計達 102.74
億元，相較於 3Q25 累計之 27.96 億元大幅增加 74.78 億元
(YoY+267.45%) 。3Q26 已進入量產，下一階段擴產將自 2Q27 啟
動，規劃自 2Q27 起每季開出一層全新無塵室加入量產，後續樓
層無塵室擴建將以現有主要客戶需求為優先配合對象。預估欣
銓 2027 年稅後 EPS 13.20 元。龍潭廠新專案是 2026~2027 年最
重要的增長引擎。而旗下全智湖口新廠，也於 3Q26 正式進入矽
光子相關新產品的量產階段，都將帶動公司中長期營收獲利向
上，因此建議欣銓 Buy ，目標價 322 元。  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 3.01 元：龍潭新廠歷經 1H26 客戶驗證與進場裝
機後，於 07/2026 起正式進入量產階段，帶動欣銓營收成長，
3Q26 營收為 53.37 億元， QoQ+17.72% 。營收依應用別分析，通
訊 Communication/Connectivity 佔 41.0% ；車用 / 安控
(ATV/Security) 佔 17.9% ；射頻晶片 (RF IC) 佔 10.9% ；微處理器
(General MCU) 佔 8.7%；Storage 佔 9.5%；PC/Consumers 佔 6.6%；
Memory 佔 3.7% ；其他 1.8% 。毛利率的部份， 3Q26 毛利率
45.43% ，增加 5.27 個百分點。 3Q26 稅後 EPS 3.01 元。  
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 3.03 元：受到 3Q26 營收基期墊高、歐美
客戶於 11/2026 至 12/2026 進入假期拉貨淡季 ， 以及手機與通訊
終端產品季節性調節，營收僅小幅成長，毛利率展望方面，由
於 4Q26 未有新無塵室開出，且公司需提前為 2027 年擴產招募
人力、提列初期整備成本與設備折舊增加，預期 4Q26 毛利率將
略低於 3Q26 之 45.43% ，但仍可穩健維持高檔水準；公司努力
進行成本管控和改善產品組合 ， 讓 4Q26 獲利有機會高於 3Q26。
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 3.03 元。 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.28 元。

2 群益投顧 
  欣銓 ESG ：  
欣銓積極推動  ESG( 環境、社會、公司治理 )相關政策與行動，並於官方網站
設有專屬  ESG 頁面與歷年永續報告書，公開揭露永續發展成果與目標。  
環境 (Environmental) 
•持續推動再生能源與減碳：延續加入  RE100 的承諾，積極推動節能減碳
與再生能源採購，並簽署 SBTi( 科學基礎減碳目標倡議 )承諾，強化減碳目標
與國際接軌。  
•循環經濟新提案：攜手供應鏈夥伴推動循環箱再進化，落實資源再利用與
減廢。  
•生物多樣性與生態尊重：持續舉辦淨灘、認養友善田等活動，強調尊重生
態、友善健康。  
社會 (Social) 
•同仁參與與文化深化：舉辦「 ESG Award 」專案競賽，鼓勵員工提出永續點
子與專案，涵蓋綠色製造、責任供應鏈、多元包容職場、人才培育、關懷弱
勢等五大方向。  
• 員工關懷與職場環境：持續改善員工宿舍環境，提升同仁生活品質。  
•社會公益：贊助在地傳統藝文活動、捐助弱勢團體，並與產業園區合作舉
辦移工籃球賽，促進多元共融。  
公司治理 (Governance) 
•資訊透明與永續報告：發行永續報告書，持續自主揭露非財務資訊，強調
資訊透明與溝通成效。  
•國際認證與標準：完成 RBA VAP 8.0 改版訓練，強化人權、環保等國際標
準合規與超越法規要求。  
•永續獎項肯定：榮獲「英國標準協會 (BSI)ESG 永續發展領航獎」及「台灣
企業永續獎 (TCSA) －永續報告獎金獎」 ，肯定公司在永續發展、資訊揭露與
社會責任的表現。  
攜手合作夥伴擴大永續影響力，推動以大帶小、標竿學習。展現穩健落實永
續目標與全球標準接軌的實力。  
◼CDP 氣候問卷榮獲  A-List 最高肯定 ： 公司在2025 年  CDP 全球環境評
比中，從全球逾  22,100 家企業脫穎而出，「氣候變遷」問卷榮獲 A 級領
導等級，「水安全」蟬聯  A- 領導等級肯定。  
◼ SBTi 近程目標通過驗證：公司設定的近程減碳目標已於 04/2026 正式通
過科學基礎減量目標倡議 (SBTi) 官方驗證。  
◼連續  9 年榮獲「公司治理評鑑前  5% 」的殊榮。  
◼龍潭廠榮獲「 2026 台灣建築永續獎金獎」肯定。  
欣銓 3Q26 營收 53.37 億元， QoQ+17.72% ，稅前淨利 18.25 億元，
QoQ+25.16% ，稅後 EPS 3.01 元：

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 28 日

3 群益投顧 
由於全球  AI 需求極其暢旺，許多一線封測同業將產能高度集中在  AI 核
心晶片，導致其他「 AI 周邊晶片」或「非  AI 通訊晶片」的測試產能出現
缺口。欣銓因此獲得大量的外溢訂單 (轉單 )，特別是在通訊與 PC Consumer 
領域。欣銓 3Q26 營收為 53.37 億元， QoQ+17.72% 。  
營收依應用別分析，通訊 Communication/Connectivity 佔 41.0% ；車用 /安控
(ATV/Security) 佔 17.9%； 射頻晶片(RF IC) 佔 10.9%； 微處理器(General MCU)
佔 8.7% ； Storage 佔 9.5% ； PC/Consumers 佔 6.6% ； Memory 佔 3.7% ；其他
1.8% 。  
3Q26 終端應用結構，通訊與連網 (Communication/Connectivity) 為最大成長
引擎，營收佔比自 2Q26 的 33.4% 大幅擴張至 41.0% 。這部份含光通訊和晶
圓代工廠的訂單 。 車用與安控(ATV/Security) 營收佔比為 17.9%，略 低 於2Q26
的 18.2% ，不過營收金額是增加，車用市場回溫，營收持續向上。射頻晶片
(RF IC) 營收佔比為 10.9% ，低於 2Q26 的 12.2% ，因為手機拉貨動能下滑。
儲存裝置 (Storage) 營收佔比自 2Q26 的 10.8% 收斂至 9.5% ，因晶圓代工廠的
產能受限。通用型微控制器 (General MCU) 佔比為 8.7% ，相較 2Q26 之 8.5%
微幅增加。個人電腦與消費性晶片 (PC/Consumers) 佔比為 6.6% ，相較 2Q26
之 9.9% 明顯下降，需求受到終端價格上漲影響，加上客戶產品組合上也會
優先供應通訊產品，營收佔比下滑較明顯。記憶體 (Memory) 營收佔比為
3.7% ，低於 2Q26 的 5.4% 。其他類別佔比則由 1.60% 微升至 1.80% 。  
毛利率的部份，得益於通訊連網晶片拉貨動能強勁以及整體稼動率維持高
檔約 80%。營業毛利達 24.25 億元， QoQ+33.1% ， YoY+89.1% ，毛利率攀升
至 45.43% ，較 2Q26 增加 5.27 個百分點，高稼動率帶動規模經濟發酵。營
業利益達 18.6 億元， QoQ+35.0% ，營益率達 34.8% ，較 2Q26 成長 4.4 個百
分點，較 3Q25 大幅增加 10.5 個百分點。業外收支部分， 3Q26 業外淨支出
為 0.34 億元，稅前淨利 18.25 億元， QoQ+25.16% ，稅後 EPS 3.01 元。創下
單季獲利新高紀錄。  
累計前三季 (1Q26 至 3Q26) 合併營收達 138.54 億元， YoY+35.4% ，毛利率達
41.7% ，營業利益達 43.34 億元， YoY+69.60% ，營益率達 31.3% ，累計稅後
淨利為 35.54 億元， YoY+78.4% ，前三季累計 EPS 達 7.49 元。  
就客戶類型分析 ，1Q26 至 3Q26 累計營收中 ， 無晶圓廠IC 設計業者 (Fabless)
貢獻 52.5% ，垂直整合製造商 (IDM) 佔 42.1% ，晶圓代工廠 (Foundry) 佔比由
前三季累計的 1.8% 提高至 5.4%，其他客戶佔比為 0.1%。在產品測試型態方
面，前三季晶圓測試 (Wafer Sorting) 營收佔比達 73.2% ，成品測試 (Final Test)
佔比為 26.1% ，其他項目佔 0.7% 。  
基於與策略客戶已談定的  ASIC 合作案，預估欣銓 2026 年資本支出將明
顯增加至約 130 億元， 2027 年將會高於 2026 年：  
欣銓對資本支出一向保持保守與謹慎，主因是高額折舊若遇上稼動率下滑，
會嚴重衝擊毛利率。此次  2026 年轉向積極擴張，主要來自與策略客戶已確
定的  ASIC 合作案。欣銓 2024 至 2025 年的資本支出主要集中在龍潭新廠
的廠房興建與土建尾款； 2026 年則轉向以「機台設備」為主，廠房支出比
例將顯著降低。先進測試機台交期長達 6 - 8 個月。目前測試機台約 1,700
台以上。  
欣銓在 1Q26 至 3Q26 期間取得不動產 、 廠房及設備(資本支出 )累計達 102.74

4 群益投顧 
億元，相較於 3Q25 累計之 27.96 億元大幅增加 74.78 億元， YoY+267.45% ，
資本支出顯著放量主要由兩大核心動能驅動：第一為龍潭新廠無塵室與基
礎設施建置，第二為子公司全智科技為配合美系通訊大客戶強勁 AI 晶片測
試需求而積極採購高階測試設備。 4Q26 因實際投入量產的機台數量相對有
限，單季資本支出將較 3Q26 持平或微幅縮減，預估 2026 年資本支出約 130
億元。但 2027 年資本支出規模掌握度高且明確將超越 2026 年。主因龍潭新
廠單一樓層無塵室與機台設備支出即達 40 億元以上，公司規劃自 2Q27 起
每季開出 1 層全新無塵室加入量產， 2027 年約有 3 個樓層陸續投產，且後
續樓層的無塵室擴建將以現有主要客戶需求為優先配合對象。  
截至 3Q26 底，公司不動產、廠房及設備帳面價值擴張至 308.94 億元，佔總
資產比重達 64% ，資產總計同步推升至 485.56 億元。  
為支應擴產資金需求，公司除於 1Q26 至 3Q26 透過銀行借款淨增 61.43 億
元外，子公司於 09/2026 處分欣銓股票取得約 40 億元流動資金，後續將以
股利形式匯回母公司以支應資本支出。為因應 2027 年更為龐大的資本支出
銓現階段仍以銀行借款為優先籌資工具，預期負債比率將自 3Q26 底的 50%
進一步拉升，但公司承諾將整體負債比率嚴格控制在 60% 以內，並視擴產階
段適時評估其他籌資工具；同時因擴建資金留存考量，現金股利配發率預計
將由 2026 年的 70% 常態區間下修至 2027 年的 50% 以上。  
欣銓在海外生產基地的布局策略上，維持專注鞏固台灣、新加坡及中國南京
等現有廠區的營運效率與產能發揮。管理層明確指出，赴新國家或地區設立
生產基地必須具備足夠的在地客戶基礎與充沛的訂單量支撐，才能避免新
廠陷入長期營運虧損；基於當前尚未取得足額訂單承諾的考量，欣銓目前並
無赴美國投資建廠的計畫。  
在現有海外據點的產品與客戶定位方面，高階 AI ASIC 與先進製程相關的
測試訂單全數集中於台灣本地廠區，包括龍潭廠及全智科湖口廠。新加坡廠
則以承接成熟產品線為主，產品結構涵蓋車用、工控及消費性晶片，主要原
因在於新加坡當地目前尚無大量先進晶圓代工產能；後續欣銓在新加坡廠
區的成長策略，將聚焦於進一步深化與聯電 (UMC) 及世界先進 (VIS) 在新加
坡當地晶圓廠的委外測試合作機會。  
折舊費用方面，欣銓已公告自 1/1/2025 起，部分機器設備耐用年限由 7 年
延長至 9 年，這將有助於反映資產實際經濟效益，欣銓 2025 年折舊費用
31.39 億元。龍潭廠量產機台之折舊費用已有部分於 3Q26 開始提列，但因
部分設備驗收時點落於季中之後，折舊並未全額反映在 3Q26 單季，預期
4Q26 折舊費用將進一步高於 3Q26 ，預估 2026 年折舊費用約 35 億元。進入
2027 年後，新設備折舊將隨各季機台進場與驗收進度按部就班提列；依產
業慣例，新機台通常需於投產前一季進場進行裝機與驗證，例如 2Q27 投產
之產能將於 1Q27 進場裝機，驗收完成後即開始提列折舊。儘管折舊費用持
續墊高且 4Q26 至 1Q27 提前招募人力與產能整備將使毛利率自 3Q26 的
45.43% 高點略為回落，但因龍潭廠 4Q26 產能利用率維持滿載、全智湖口新
廠產能將於 2027 年達全產全銷，產能高稼動率預期可有效稀釋固定折舊成
本，使單季毛利率持續穩健維持在 40% 以上。  
產能利用率已達滿載，預估欣銓 4Q26 營收 55.24 億元， QoQ+3.50% ，稅前
淨利 18.57 億元， QoQ+1.75% ，稅後 EPS 3.03 元：  
展望 4Q26 營運，欣銓預估合併營收將呈現 QoQ+3% 左右，主要受到 3Q26

5 群益投顧 
營收基期墊高、歐美客戶於 11/2026 至 12/2026 進入假期拉貨淡季，以及手
機與通訊終端產品季節性調節所致。  
旗下全智總坪數逾 3,500 坪的湖口新廠，也於 3Q26 正式進入矽光子相關新
產品的量產階段，預期 4Q26 可以開出 20-25% 的產能，全智湖口廠客戶需
求高，因此 2027 年可以達到滿載狀況。  
毛利率展望方面，由於 4Q26 未有新無塵室開出，且公司需提前為 2027 年
擴產招募人力、提列初期整備成本與設備折舊增加，預期 4Q26 毛利率將略
低於 3Q26 之 45.43% ，但仍可穩健維持在高檔水準。定價策略方面，欣銓對
主力客戶價格維持穩定，但已針對低毛利與零星客戶提出調價，部分客戶已
順利反映漲價。公司努力進行成本管控和改善產品組合，讓 4Q26 獲利有機
會高於 3Q26。預估欣銓 4Q26 營收 55.24 億元， QoQ+3.50% ，稅前淨利 18.57
億元， QoQ+1.75% ，稅後 EPS 3.03 元。  
預估欣銓 2026 年營收 193.78 億元， YoY+38.13% ，稅前淨利 63.05 億元，
YoY+74.58% ，稅後 EPS 10.28 元。  
欣銓提供完整的光電測試 (全智 )與先進異質整合封裝 (瑞峰 )解決方案，建構
完整 CPO 生態系：  
欣銓集團在矽光子領域的布局完整，透過結合母公司與旗下轉投資公司的
資源，建構完整 CPO( 共封裝光學 )生態系，欣銓與全智皆為  SEMI 矽光子
產業聯盟及異質整合矽光子產業聯盟的重要成員。欣銓已布局光通訊與矽
光子 (CPO) 高階測試架構，目前接洽並配合之客戶達三家以上，測試製程至
少已切入至 Insertion 1 和 3 階段，並持續往 Insertion 4 進行技術評估與了
解。由於目前全球矽光子晶片出貨量仍處於技術開發與初期工程驗證階段，
市場尚未進入大規模營收爆發期，因此公司尚未設定具體之 CPO 營收貢獻
目標，但已透過全智湖口廠與美系通訊核心客戶建立緊密夥伴關係，提早卡
位高速傳輸光電整合封裝之長期測試商機。  
欣銓集團的矽光子佈局中，全智科技 (欣銓 100% 轉投資 )主要負責  EIC 測
試、PIC 測試以及光電整合測試 。 其中 EIC 測試已進入量產多年 ， 並於 2024 
年建置矽光子專用的光電整合測試實驗室，提供全方位的工程測試與驗證
服務。瑞峰半導體 (欣銓投資約  25%) 專注於先進異質整合封裝 (如 CPO 3D 
TSV/CoW ， EIC+PIC) 。瑞峰負責將 EIC 晶圓疊加在 PIC 晶圓上 (Chip on 
Wafer) ，隨後再交由全智進行光電整合測試。 Fabrinet( 策略投資夥伴 )主要負
責後續的  CPO 相關製程與光學引擎等項目。  
全智 (湖口新廠 )3Q26 正式量產，總面積超過  3,500 坪的湖口新廠，將著重
於矽光子測試。根據客戶端訊息，該廠區矽光子新產品的產能利用率拉升速
度預期將會相當快，且已與客戶洽談，有機會在  2027 年接近滿載。在  CPO 
的  EIC/PIC 測試產能上，目前已佈建超過  150 台設備。中長期擴廠計畫，
為因應強勁的光通訊客戶需求，湖口廠目前正在整理無塵室空間，希望能再
騰出 1~2 層樓來配合擴充；同時，為 2028 年以後的長期成長，公司也已準
備著手尋求購買新土地。  
瑞峰已於  2024 年建置第一條  12 吋矽光子晶圓級封裝 (WLP) 生產線。目
前將  EIC 疊在  PIC 晶圓上的  Chip on Wafer 製程正逐漸進入量產。 2025 
年已導入數家新客戶，進行超過  30 項  NPI( 新產品導入 )驗證； 2026 年更

預計會有超過  10 家新客戶 (Tape-out / New design) 進行導入與設計中。  
預估欣銓 2027 年營收 251.13 億元， YoY+29.60% ，稅前淨利 81.39 億元，
YoY+29.08% ，稅後 EPS 13.20 元：  
展望 2027 年營運前景，欣銓管理層對整體營收增長維持正面樂觀基調，主
要受惠於 2 大核心擴產引擎全面啟動：第一為龍潭新廠無塵室自 2Q27 起進
入逐季開出階段，第二為子公司全智科技新竹湖口新廠產能將有機會達到
全產全銷。  
除晶圓代工廠外包訂單外，美系通訊核心客戶對高階 AI 晶片測試需求自
4Q26 一路強勁延伸至 2027 全年，全智湖口新廠產能已獲客戶全數包下，若
產能利用率達到滿載運作下，預期有機會貢獻 2027 年單月營收 1~1.5 億元。
車用與安控 (ATV/Security) 及儲存裝置 (Storage) 受惠於 Edge AI 與資料中心
需求回溫， 2027 年營收貢獻將持續擴張。  
季度走勢上，延續 4Q26 營收 QoQ+1~3% 的走勢， 1Q27 受到農曆春節工作
天數減少與季節性影響，預估營收將持平於 4Q26 ，毛利率受前期準備費用
影響維持在 40% 以上高檔水準，整體產能利用率 (UTR) 則維持約 80% 健康水
位。真正的成長將自 2Q27 啟動，欣銓龍潭廠共有 7 層無塵室，第 1 層已於
2Q26 年完成認證，自 07/2026 正式開始貢獻營收與量產。第 2 至第 3 層無
塵室工程預計於 2026 年底至 1H27 完工，其中第 2 層產能最快自 2Q27 開始
貢獻營運，依每季啟用一層的節奏逐步推進。  
獲利能力與定價方面，雖然新廠開出初期伴隨折舊與人力整備等成本，但隨
著 AI 測試需求暢旺帶動整體產能利用率持續站穩高檔，加上高階晶圓測試
比重維持高檔，評估 2027 年毛利率將高於 2026 年。此外，公司針對主要長
期配合之大型客戶維持穩定報價，但對於低毛利與零星客戶已啟動調價提
議並陸續反映漲價，進一步強化整體產品組合獲利體質。  
預估欣銓 2027 年營收 251.13 億元， YoY+29.60% ，稅前淨利 81.39 億元，
YoY+29.08% ，稅後 EPS 13.20 元。

個股報告

10 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

◼ 結 論 與 建 議 
基 於 以 下 理 由 ， 研究部 維持 力成 買 進 評 等  
(1) 記 憶 體 、 高 階 邏 輯 封 測 需 求 強 勁 ， 營 收 及 獲 利 創 歷 年 單 季 新 高 ， 目 前封
裝 / 測 試 稼 動 率 皆 超 過 90% ， 加 上 產 品 調 價 與 產 品 組 合 改 善 ， 4 Q26 營 收 、
毛 利 率 仍 有 成 長 空 間  
(2) 目 前 稼 動 率 接 近 滿 載 ， DRA M 需 求 持 續 大 於 供 給 ， NA ND 受 惠 企 業 及
SSD 需 求 增 加 ， 邏 輯 業 務 亦 維 持 雙 位 數 成 長 。 且 隨 載 板 等 材 料 成 本 上 升 ，
力 成 依 不 同 產 品 向 客 戶 反 映 成 本 ， 漲 價 幅 度 介 於 個 位 數 至 雙 位 數 ， 有 助 於
維 持 毛 利 率 改 善 趨 勢  
(3) 目 前 包 含 Flip Chip BGA 等 先 進 封 裝 相 關 業 務 已 占 集 團 營 收 30% 以 上 ，
FOPLP 客 戶 及 產 品 種 類 逐 漸 明 確 ， 設 備 取 得 情 況 亦 較 先 前 改 善 。 隨 客 戶 驗
證 完 成 及 量 產 規 模 擴 大 ， 預 期 先 進 封 裝 將 成 為 202 7 年 後 主 要 成 長 動 能  
(4) 與 AMD 、 Broad com 等 大 客 戶 推 進 先 進 封 裝 合 作 ， 布 局 FOPL P 、 CPO
等 技 術 ， 帶 動 2027 年 後 營 收 結 構 轉 型 機 會 。

4Q26 維 持 成 長 ， 2027 年 FOP LP 進 入 量 產  
4Q26  DRA M 需 求 持 續 大 於 供 給 ， 封 測 訂 單 能 見 度 維 持 高 檔 ； NA ND 則受
惠 AI 伺 服 器 及 企 業 級 S SD 需 求 增 加 。 由 於 目 前 封 裝 與 測 試 稼 動 率 均 超 過
90% ， 預 期 4Q26 在 客 戶 需 求 增 加 下 ， 產 能 利 用 率 有 望 進 一 步 提 升 。 在價
格 方面 ， 近期 因 Substra te 等 原 材 料 成 本 上 漲 ， 力 成 已 依 不 同 產 品 及 成 本
變 化 向 客 戶 反 映 價 格 ， 漲 幅 約 介 於 個 位 數 至 雙 位 數 ， 且 客 戶 已 接 受 漲價 。
隨 漲 價 效 益 持 續 反 映 ， 以 及 產 能 逐 步 轉 向 高 附 加 價 值 產 品 ， 有 助 維 持 毛 利
率 改 善 趨 勢 。 高 階 邏 輯 方 面 ，Flip Chip BGA 於 3Q26 導入 7 5 × 7 8 m m大尺
寸 封 裝 ， 4Q26 進 一 步 導 入 7 8 × 8 8 m m規 格 ， 整 合 SoC 與 D RA M 等 元 件 ，
持 續 擴 大 AI ／ HPC 產 品 布 局 。  
2027 年 成 長 動 能 由 既 有 記 憶 體 封 測 延 伸 至 高 階 邏 輯 及 先 進 封 裝 ， 營 收 結 構
逐 步 改 善 。  記 憶 體 方 面 ， 力成 透 過 設 備 汰 換 、 既 有 產 能 優 化 及 高 階 產 品 導
入 提 升 產 出 效 率 。 邏 輯 方 面 ， AI ／ HPC 及 高 階 測 試 需 求 增 加 ， 超 豐 與 Tera 
Probe ／ Ter aPower 持 續 擴 充 相 關 產 能 ， 以 配 合 客 戶 新 產 品 需 求 。 先 進 封
裝 方 面 ， FOPL P 持 續 進 行 客 戶 驗 證 ， 朝 2027 年 量 產 目 標 推 進 ， 設 備 取 得
情況 亦 有 所 改 善 ，2027 ～ 2028 年 擴 產 計 畫 維 持 原 定 進 度 。 隨 新 產 品 陸 續 導
入 ， 以 及 先 進 封 裝 業 務 規 模 擴 大 ， 預 期 可 進 一 步 降 低 集 團 對 傳 統 記 憶 體 封

評等說明  
1)  強力 買進 / Str on g  Bu y ： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於 50 % 
2)  買進 / Bu y： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於 25%  
3)  逢低 買進 /Tr a d in g  Bu y ： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 小於 25 % 
4)  未評等 / N ot Ra te d ： 至 目 前 我 們 沒 有 足 夠 基 本 資 料 判 斷 該 公 司 評 等 ； 或 此 期 間 康 和 與 該 公 司 有 特 定 交 易 ， 基 於 規 範 或 康
和 政 策 ， 限 制 予 該 公 司 評 等

免責宣言 :  
本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 康 和 投 資 顧 問 股 份 有 限 公 司 版 權 所 有 ， 禁 止 任 何 形 式 之 抄 襲 、 引 用 或 轉 載 。 本 研 究 報 告 所 載 內 容 ， 純 屬
研 究 性 質 ， 所 載 述 意 見 可 隨 時 予 以 更 改 或 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 ， 僅 提 供 康 和 集 團 內 部 同 仁 及 客 戶 參 考 ， 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何
意 見 ， 不 構 成 ( 且 不 應 解 釋 為 ) 在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 劵 或 其 他 投 資 標 的 之 要 約 、 招 攬 、 邀 請 、 宣 傳 、 誘 使 ，

精測 (6510 TT) 前 三 季 大 賺 四 股 本。精 測 受 惠 於 HPC 測 試 需 求 ，3Q26 營 收 、獲 利 皆 創

dan.chuang@cim.capital.com.tw

精測 3Q26 獲 利 季 增 、 年 增：  
測試介面大廠精測 10/27 公布 3Q26 營收 19.05 億元，
QoQ+16.17%/YoY+53.35% ；毛 利 率 57.3% ；營 益 率 35 .2 %；稅 後 純 益 5.51 億
元， QoQ+11.61%/YoY+100.03% ；稅後 EPS 16.75 元 。 前 三 季 歸 屬 母 公 司 業
主 合 併 淨 利 13.87 億 元 ， 年 增 逾 90% ， 每 股 純 益 42.2 元 ， 大 賺 逾 四 個 股 本 。 
精測 3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 皆 創 單 季 新 高：  
精 測 表 示 ，3Q26 營 收 、 獲 利 等 財 務 指 標 均 創 單 季 新 高 ， 並 且 連 續 三 季 賺 逾
一 個 股 本，充 分 展 現 公 司 在 產 品 組 合、成 本 效 率 與 客 戶 需 求 的 競 爭 優 勢 持 續
強 化 ， 為 後 續 成 長 奠 定 穩 固 基 礎 。 
AI 帶 動 晶 片 朝 向高 效 能 、 高 頻 寬 與 高 功 率 架 構 發 展， 精 測 產 品 線 完 整：  
精 測 指 出 ， 隨 著 AI 應 用 全 面 擴 散 ， 推 動 晶 片 朝 高 效 能 、 高 頻 寬 與 高 功 率 架
構 發 展，測 試 複 雜 度 與 技 術 門 檻 同 步 提 高，客 戶 對 高 階 測 試 介 面 的 需 求 日 益
迫 切 ， 該 公 司 已 推 出 AI 與 高 效 能 運 算 （HPC ） 完 整 測 試 介 面 產 品 ， 涵 蓋 晶
圓 測 試 探 針 卡、高 功 率 老 化 測 試 板、IC 測 試 載 板 及 系 統 級 測 試 板 解 決 方 案，
以 滿 足 客 戶 一 次 購 足 的 需 求 。 
精測 完 整 導 入 智 慧 製 造 ， 在 產 能 擴 張 下 ， 有 助 維 持 獲 利 率：  
精 測 強 調 ， 公 司 同 步 精 進 研 發 與 製 造 流 程 ， 導 入AI 智 慧 製 造 ， 以 提 升 製 程
技 術 與 生 產 效 率，強 化 產 品 開 發 時 程 與 品 質 管 理 能 力，使 淨 利 率 在 產 能 擴 張
下 仍 穩 健 維 持 高 檔 水 準 。 
精測 4Q26 營運 審 慎 樂 觀 ：  
精 測 指 出，未 來 將 持 續 依 客 戶 需 求 與 市 場 變 化，審 慎 配 置 資 源，深 化 客 戶 合
作 關 係 。 研 發 重 點 聚 焦 下 一 世 代 探 針 卡 與 測 試 載 板 ， 以 因 應AI 客 製 化 晶 片
需 求 ， 同 時 推 動 「AI in House 」 企 業 智 慧 協 作 ， 探 索 AI 於 研 發 設 計 、 工 程
知 識 管 理 、 供 應 鏈 協 作 及 智 慧 製 造 等 應 用 ， 審 慎 樂 觀4Q26 及 未 來 營 運 。 
投 資 建 議 ：

望季增， 2027 年 展 望 正 向 ， 投 資 建 議 維 持 Buy 。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 2

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

1 
Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and

Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

Document 255: Bank Sector Outlook 2027_ High supply meets a changing financial landscape

Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 38
Status: ok

### Page 1

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

28 October 2026

www.ing.com/THINK

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 
October 2026

THINK Economic and Financial Analysis

High supply meets a changing financial 
landscape

### Page 2

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Summary 
European banks face another year of high bond supply in 2027, alongside growing 
sustainable issuance and a shifting financial landscape shaped by AT1s, stablecoins 
and regulatory reform 
• Bank bond supply. We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down 
slightly from the €455bn forecast for 2026. Redemptions remain the main driver of 
the high supply, but the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected to push 
supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance reaching €185bn, senior 
unsecured bond supply at €210bn and another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds. 
• Sustainable bank bond supply set for further growth in 2027. Banks issued a 
higher-than-expected amount of sustainable debt in 2026 despite Europe’s ESG 
deregulation efforts. We expect sustainable bank bond supply to continue rising, 
reaching €100bn in 2027, up 7.5% from 2026. Green issuance will remain dominant 
at €85bn, with 20% issued under the EU Green Bond Standard (EuGBS). Sustainable 
loan origination, higher redemptions, revisions to the EU Taxonomy’s technical 
screening criteria and resilient investor demand will continue to support ESG 
issuance. 
• Bank capital – Why AT1 still matters in 2027. AT1 bank bonds will remain an 
important part of European banks’ capital stacks in 2027, with meaningful reform 
still on the back burner. Bonds being called at their first call date remains our base 
case, despite extension risk having risen recently. We expect EUR AT1 supply to rise 
to €20bn, with EUR Tier 2 increasing to €35bn, largely driven by refinancing, while net 
supply is relatively modest. Valuations have improved but still remain at the tighter 
end, while risks are becoming more visible. The yield should continue to support 
demand. Lower reset levels, renewed sovereign-bank concerns, and a softening of 
bank fundamentals could bring more issuer and structure dispersion next year 
• M&A. European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and 2027 should 
stay busy. Domestic deals still dominate, while cross-border consolidation is harder 
but is also picking up as the Unicredit-Commerzbank transaction has shown. 
Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and execution still matter. Expect 
more deals, but a transformation of Europe's banking sector is still some way off. 
• Stablecoins. Stablecoins' market capitalisation grew significantly over the past two 
years, and with this has come widespread scrutiny. Upon broad adoption, the crypto 
asset could have multiple impacts, including on European banks. This could imply a 
shift in their funding base, negative impact on credit provisions, as well as spillover 
risks. However, the sector could also benefit from the technology, notably by issuing 
its own tokens. Several regulatory changes expected in 2027 could alter the impact 
of stablecoins on banks. Despite the multiple risks identified, one should not expect 
banks to surrender ground to fintech issuers without a fight. 
• Solvency II reforms: limited support for bank bonds, while securitisation gains 
some ground. The Solvency II amendments will apply from 30 January 2027, making 
the reforms a key regulatory theme for 2027. A central aim is to free up capital and 
promote long-term investments by European insurers, supporting Europe’s Savings 
and Investment Union. This could also give insurers greater scope to invest in bonds. 
However, we don’t expect any material reallocation towards bank bonds. 
Securitisations should benefit most directly from a more favourable capital 
treatment, while covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should benefit mainly 
indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers.

### Page 3

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

High redemption volumes are expected to keep EUR bank bond supply elevated in 2027

Variables driving next year's bank bond supply 
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down slightly from the 
€455bn forecast for 2026. In our view, several variables will be steering next year’s 
issuance. 
Redemptions will remain the central driver of the elevated 2027 bank bond supply 
forecast. Nearly €400bn of bonds mature in 2027, up €30bn year-on-year and the 
highest level in a decade. The increase is especially significant in the covered bond 
segment where €186bn will mature, a historic high. 
The subordinated segment will also rise, with redemptions totalling €45bn, up about 
€6bn from 2026. Of this total, €31bn is in Tier 2 instruments and €14bn in AT1s. The 
senior unsecured segment will partially offset these increases, with total redemptions 
reaching more than €162bn, down from more than €170bn recorded this year. The 
majority stems from senior bail-in bonds (senior non-preferred or senior HoldCo) with 
more than €90bn and an additional €71bn maturing in senior preferred instruments. 
Despite diverging trends across segments, elevated redemption volumes remain the 
main driver of bank issuance in the euro bond primary market, as institutions refinance 
maturing instruments.

Marine Leleux 
Sector Strategist, Financials 
marine.leleux2@ing.com

Jesse Norcross 
Senior Sector Strategist, Real Estate 
jesse.norcross@ing.com

Bank bond supply to stay at cruising 
altitude in 2027  
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027. Redemptions remain the 
main driver of the high supply, but the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected 
to push supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance reaching €185bn, 
senior unsecured bond supply at €210bn and another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds

### Page 4

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Bank bond redemptions per segment

Source: ING

While high redemption levels point to stronger supply in 2027, other factors also play a 
role, such as the macroeconomic environment. Eurozone GDP growth is forecast to 
increase slightly in the second half of next year to reach 1.5%, still well below levels seen 
after the pandemic. 
Furthermore, our economists pencil in an additional European Central Bank rate hike in 
2026, bringing policy rates to 2.75% in early 2027. The higher interest rate environment 
is not expected to support lending growth, especially as geopolitical instability remains. 
As a result, we do not see the modest macroeconomic backdrop acting as a catalyst for 
bank bond supply in 2027. 
Policy rates and GDP growth forecasts

Source: ING

Asset swap spread levels initially proved very resilient to geopolitical instability and 
related economic changes this year. Despite some volatility in March, following the 
outbreak of the US-Israel conflict with Iran, spread levels recovered quickly and reached 
levels close to their historic low. However, a broader escalation of the conflict across the 
Middle East, coupled with the absence of a clear path to resolution, could put further 
pressure on spreads. 
In addition, budget and political concerns in France and OAT-Bund volatility, as well as 
broader European fiscal concerns, have recently led to spreads widening again. The 
combination of French political uncertainty and the Middle East conflict could trigger 
some periods of volatility in the market in 2027. However, we believe these uncertainties 
will not be a strong negative for bank bond supply. Depending on the timing, though,

### Page 5

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

periodic widening may make financial institutions less keen to pre-fund redemptions 
coming up in 2028. 
Our 2027 supply forecast 
When accounting for these three main variables, we expect the EUR bank bond supply 
to reach €450bn in 2027. This translates into a net supply of €57bn, down from €89bn in 
2026. 
Forecast EUR bank bond supply for 2027

YTD = 16/10 
Source: ING

Our estimate includes €185bn in covered bonds, comprising €180bn in benchmark-sized 
issuances, including floating rate notes, and €5bn in sub-benchmark bonds. This is a 
€5bn drop versus our 2026 forecast. It reflects the pre-financing already made by issuers 
this year in preparation for the historically high redemptions. 
Furthermore, next year’s mortgage lending forecasts point to stagnation, somewhat 
limiting any further increase in covered bond issuance. Besides, with the securitisation 
package entering its finalisation stage, issuers with high funding needs may shift some 
secured issuance towards securitisation for diversification purposes. Increasing demand 
for covered bonds from overseas investors driven by regulatory developments may also 
result in more currency diversification, away from EUR-denominated issuance. 
We forecast senior unsecured supply to reach €210bn next year. This is split between 
€70bn in senior preferred bonds and €140bn in senior bail-in instruments. It represents a 
€10bn YoY decline in senior preferred supply, reflecting both the drop in the segment’s 
redemptions from 2027 onwards and the potential impact of the Crisis Management 
and Deposit Insurance (CMDI) framework. As the CMDI reforms are expected to enter 
into force in 2028, they could reduce the attractiveness of senior preferred bonds versus 
senior bail-in instruments in some EU countries, as senior preferred exposures will rank 
lower than, and no longer pari passu to, uncovered deposits in the creditor hierarchy. 
The funding cost advantage of senior preferred unsecured issuance could decline. At the 
same time, issuing senior non-preferred bonds could serve the purpose of increasing the 
bail-inable layer protecting preferred senior bonds and their current ratings. This could 
shift some issuers’ focus towards the senior non-preferred bucket instead of the senior 
preferred segment. 
Our forecast for senior non-preferred supply remains stable compared to our 2026 
estimate. While redemptions will increase by €6bn in 2027 and further in 2028 to reach 
€102bn, we believe the impact of higher refinancing needs on non-preferred senior 
supply will be partially offset by the lack of balance sheet growth against the backdrop 
of higher interest rates. Furthermore, the outperformance of US dollar debt versus EUR-

### Page 6

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

denominated debt could see issuers turn more often towards the USD market, 
potentially negatively affecting EUR senior bail-in supply. 
Lastly, we expect Tier 2 supply to reach €35bn and AT1 supply to reach €20bn in 2027. 
This is a €5bn increase YoY in both AT1 and Tier 2 compared to our 2026 forecast, mainly 
driven by higher redemption levels. With AT1 redemptions in 2027 increasing by €3bn 
YoY to €14bn, net supply is likely to be marginally higher next year. In Tier 2, 
redemptions increase by €4bn to €31bn, essentially meaning that net supply will only 
be marginally higher. 
As the chart below highlights, net supply in AT1 has been significant in recent years but 
has slowed this year as the focus has shifted towards refinancing. As such, while we 
expect more AT1 supply next year compared to 2026, the levels are relatively in line 
with the longer-term trend. 
Forecast EUR AT1 supply (€bn)

Source: ING

The main driver of supply will also be refinancing for subordinated capital. We expect a 
further marginal decline in CET1 buffers, although most banks are already operating at 
target AT1 and Tier 2 ratios. Still, there are some issuers with substantial gaps in either 
AT1 or Tier 2 ratios, which they may look to improve, leading to a slight increase in 
supply. 
Significant Risk Transfer (SRT) issuance has also increased in recent years and will 
continue to grow as a tool for banks in terms of balance sheet and capital management, 
while it is also growing as an investable asset class. Although SRTs provide capital and 
RWA relief, they do not replace the gone-concern and loss-absorption capacity of AT1 
and Tier 2 instruments. As such, they play a more complementary role in capital 
management, and we do not expect it to have a major impact on bank capital issuance 
in 2027. 
Bank M&A could also be a wildcard for 2027 issuance. With several large M&A 
transactions potentially taking place, this may lead to some capital optimisation and 
less incremental supply needs; conversely, goodwill effects, potential regulatory 
increases in buffer requirements or RWA inflation may lead to a slight increase in the 
need for capital. However, on the whole, we would expect M&A to have a slight 
dampening effect on supply. 
Finally, spreads are currently quite elevated, and coupons on new issues are generally 
higher than legacy bonds maturing or callable in 2027. This may also lead to a more 
wait-and-see approach from issuers, but we still think that most issuers will continue to 
drive their capital plans as in previous years.

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Forecast EUR bank bond supply for 2026

### Page 8

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Sustainable bank bond supply is blooming, nearing a record in 2026

Banks issued an unexpectedly large amount of sustainable debt this year despite 
Europe’s ESG deregulation efforts. Up to mid-October 2026, they issued more than 
€86bn in sustainable bonds across different instrument types, up by almost €20bn from 
the same period last year. 
This was a surprisingly strong increase given that favourable primary market conditions 
offered issuers little greenium, despite continued evidence that sustainable bonds 
attract higher investor demand, as reflected in stronger oversubscription rates. This 
suggests that the availability of sustainable assets was not a meaningful constraint on 
banks' sustainable debt issuance, thanks to higher redemptions and sustainable lending 
growth. 
Issuers are also increasingly directing ESG bond issuance towards European capital 
markets, often at the expense of North American markets, reflecting the growing 
divergence in political and regulatory priorities between the two regions. 
The bulk of the YTD supply (€76.6bn) (re)financed green assets, with green supply up 
€25bn from 2025. Instead, issuance in social and sustainability formats did not exceed 
€9.6bn, trailing last year’s supply by €4.5bn. All instrument types, from secured to 
unsecured to subordinated, contributed to the higher ESG supply volumes. However, 
sustainable T2 and bail-in senior (senior non-preferred and HoldCo) issuance increased 
particularly strongly, as the funding cost advantage from sustainable issuance was 
better for these instruments than for covered bonds and preferred senior debt. 
Issuance under the European Green Bond Standard (EuGBS) rose from €2.5bn to almost 
€12bn YTD. For many banks, ICMA-aligned green bond issuance remained the more 
practical option as it allows them to finance a broader pool of assets, rather than fully 
commit the bond proceeds to Taxonomy-aligned activities. This is particularly relevant

Maureen Schuller 
Head of Financials Sector Strategy 
maureen.schuller@ing.com

Sustainable bank bond supply set for 
further growth in 2027 
Sustainable bank bond supply is close to reaching a record in 2026, despite Europe’s ESG 
simplification agenda. We expect another increase to €100bn in 2027, supported by 
sustainable lending growth, higher redemptions and resilient investor demand

when non-EU assets are financed, for which the EU regulation-based do-no-significant-
harm requirements (DNSH) are difficult to apply. 
That said, with the revisions to the EU Taxonomy’s technical screening criteria around 
the corner (expected in 4Q 2026), we expect more banks to inaugurate European Green 
Bond Programmes, as more types of green assets will become available due to the EU’s 
simplification agenda.

Source: IGM, ING

What is driving growth in ESG supply in 2027? 
We expect sustainable bank bond supply to reach €100bn in FY2027, up from €93bn in 
FY2026 and equivalent to 22% of total euro-denominated bank bond supply. Green 
issuance remains dominant at €85bn, of which €17bn (20%) will be issued under the EU 
Green Bond Standard (EuGBS). Social, sustainable and sustainability-linked supply will 
total €15bn, of which 80% will be issued in social format. 
Greater availability of social assets, together with regulatory initiatives such as the 
Affordable Housing Act presented by the European Commission in September 2026, 
should help social issuance to gain further traction in 2027. 
Green supply will reach €85bn of which 20% will be issued as EuGBs (€bn)

Includes only euro-denominated bank bonds with a minimum size of €250m  
Source: IGM, ING

Sustainable loan origination will, in general, remain an important driver of ESG supply. 
Higher interest rate levels will dampen lending growth in 2027, but sustainable lending 
will likely be impacted to a lesser extent.

### Page 10

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Borrowing for energy efficiency improvements or with a low carbon footprint could trim 
borrowing costs, while high energy prices and costs may incentivise companies and 
households to move forward with investments to reduce their energy dependence and 
use. Banks’ net-zero commitments also support the continued focus on the greening of 
their balance sheets. 
Moreover, banks will repay €67bn in EUR sustainable debt in 2027 up from €54bn in 
2026. These redemptions should release some assets for refinancing, although loan 
amortisation, look-back periods and changes in use-of-proceeds criteria may prevent a 
full refinancing with new sustainable debt. In 2026, banks replaced 70% of their 
sustainable redemptions partially or fully with new sustainable supply. A comparable 
trend for 2027 would already cover close to half of our supply estimate for next year. 
Sustainable bank bond redemptions rise to €67bn in 2027 (€bn)

Includes only euro-denominated bank bonds with a minimum size of €250m

Simplification won't stall banks' sustainable supply 
Europe’s simplification drive is not derailing sustainable bank bond supply. Growing 
sustainable loan portfolios, higher redemptions and resilient investor demand 
should support further growth in 2027, even as overall bank bond supply remains 
broadly stable.

### Page 13

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

UniCredit and Commerzbank are at the centre of Europe’s closely watched push towards cross -border banking 
consolidation

European bank M&A is back 
European bank M&A has seen a strong resurgence, with several large-scale deals 
making headlines. UniCredit’s high-stakes takeover of Commerzbank, once deemed very 
unlikely but now moving closer to completion, could be Europe’s most significant cross-
border transaction in years. Italy remains one of Europe’s bank M&A hotbeds, with 
multiple banks taking part in the ongoing consolidation wave. Other banks have also 
been active, and we expect M&A to remain elevated in 2027. 
Year-to-date M&A deal value has already surpassed the 2025 total, reaching €53bn 
including the UniCredit-Commerzbank transaction and based on deals valued at €100m or 
more. Furthermore, deals pending or intended total €39bn, which includes the potential 
takeover of Banca Monte dei Paschi by Intesa Sanpaolo, currently valued at around €34bn. 
All in, this would bring deal value back to levels not seen since the early 2010s. 
European bank M&A volumes have seen a resurgence (€bn)

Source: Refinitiv, ING

European bank M&A is back with a

European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and 2027 should stay busy. 
Domestic deals still dominate, while cross-border consolidation is harder but is also 
picking up. Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and execution still 
tt  E t  d l  b t  t f ti  f E '  b ki  t  i  till

### Page 14

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

The rationale for European bank M&A has changed. After the financial crisis, deals were 
mostly about repair, followed by a wave of deals involving stronger domestic banks 
buying weaker rivals. Today, the backdrop has shifted – profitability and capital levels 
are stronger, making the obvious cost-efficiency story less compelling on its own. 
Instead, banks are using M&A to add scale, deposits and fee-generating diversification, 
while the need to close the gap with US rivals remains a key driver. 
The industrial logic has also strengthened, as fixed costs are rising in areas such as 
technology, AI, compliance, as well as personnel. Rising investment requirements mean 
that remaining subscale is becoming more expensive, even for highly profitable banks. 
Domestic dominates, but cross-border competes 
UniCredit-Commerzbank remains the sector’s most closely watched deal and could 
become the first major cross-border acquisition since the financial crisis. UniCredit has 
slowly been building its stake rather than a clean takeover and is now deemed to hold 
effective control, despite currently owning around 50% of the shares. Furthermore, it is 
also not entirely a leap into an unfamiliar market, as UniCredit already has a significant 
banking business in Germany through its subsidiary, HVB. 
The deal has also faced considerable pushback as well as political and stakeholder 
opposition, but is now looking increasingly likely to proceed. It has highlighted the 
difficulty of pulling off a large-scale cross-border deal for European banks. Yet it is now 
becoming the clearest live test of whether Europe is genuinely ready for cross-border 
bank consolidation. 
Domestic deals remain the rule, but we have seen other examples of cross-border deals. 
These include BPCE’s acquisition of Portuguese peer Novo Banco; Erste Bank's acquisition 
of Santander Bank Polska; and Crédit Agricole acquiring IKB to increase its presence in 
Germany. Some banks are also looking across the pond, such as Santander, which 
acquired Webster Financial Corporation in the US for USD12.5bn. 
These transactions are important, but they do not yet amount to a broad-based cross-
border consolidation wave. Many involve banks already familiar with the target market 
or assets that complement an existing local business.

### Page 15

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Italy is still riding the wave 
Italy has become one of Europe’s busiest M&A markets, riding a new wave of 
consolidation, with Banca Monte dei Paschi (MPS) at the epicentre. The bank completed 
the acquisition of Mediobanca in 2025, and this year approached Banco BPM and Banca 
Generali for a merger as a counteroffensive to Intesa Sanpaolo’s hostile bid for MPS. 
The counteroffensive now appears less likely, as key shareholders have opposed parts of 
MPS’s merger plans and have also backed Intesa’s bid. The ECB has also provided 
authorisation to take control of MPS, representing an important step for the €34bn 
takeover. Alongside this, we had the BPER–Popolare di Sondrio merger earlier in the year, 
adding to Italy’s consolidation. 
Why is Italy so active in particular? Well, Italy remains quite fragmented (see below) and 
still offers a large domestic synergy pool, even after the wave of M&A that has already 
taken place. Furthermore, the country has meaningful industrial scale and offers further 
revenue diversification opportunities for banks and insurers. Although the obvious 
combinations are becoming less evident in Italy, we expect this to continue to drive M&A 
activity in 2027. 
Top 5 banks' lending market share by country (2025)

Source: AFME, ING

It's not only about getting bigger 
The deal mix is also broadening, as banks are increasingly focusing on fee income as 
well as scale, while also stepping into capital-light businesses. Deals in sectors such as 
wealth management, asset management, payments & FX and insurance are likely to 
feature prominently. 
NatWest Group’s acquisition of Evelyn Partners is a good example. The combination 
created the UK’s largest Private Banking and Wealth Management business, while lifting 
Natwest’s fee income by 20%. Similarly, BNP Paribas’s acquisition of AXA Investment 
Managers in 2025 created a €1.5tn asset management business, as the bank is seeking 
to compete with US asset management rivals and grow its fee income. In Germany, 
Deutsche Bank and DWS were reported to be exploring a potential minority investment 
in the life insurance business Frankfurter Leben. This would mark Deutsche’s return to 
life insurance after more than a decade. 
Consolidation can support bank performance 
But does consolidation actually deliver better outcomes? The evidence is not entirely 
clear-cut, but overall points in the right direction. While increasing scale is not always 
the best use of capital, in general, more consolidated markets tend to have more stable 
and, in some cases, higher profitability. The ECB is in favour of further consolidation 
where appropriate, as it sees the benefits of diversification and efficiency. On the other 
hand, capital can be reduced following a merger. This could be a downside, as better 
20%
36% 36%
51% 53% 61% 64%

100%

### Page 16

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

capitalised banks tend to enjoy lower funding costs and thus have more room to lend to 
the real economy. 
The outcome therefore depends on the trade-off between longer-term synergies and 
the upfront capital, execution and integration costs. 
ECB data show an association between more concentrated banking markets and 
profitability. As the chart below shows, countries with a high level of concentration 
(defined by a higher Herfindahl-Hirschman Index coefficient) tend to have higher net 
interest margins on average. Still, net interest margins in Europe are structurally lower 
than in the US, where they tend to be between 3–4%. 
Net interest margin vs market concentration for European countries (2025)

Source: ECB, ING

Similarly, a higher level of market concentration in European countries is associated with 
a lower cost of risk. The correlation is modest, with some clear outliers, but the numbers 
show that greater concentration can lead to lower asset quality costs, although the 
relationship should perhaps not be interpreted as causal. Still, taken together, this 
suggests that consolidation can improve banking markets. 
Cost of risk vs market concentration for European countries (2025)

Source: ECB, ING

Regulation is helping, but can get in the way 
The Banking Union has made deals easier to assess: the ECB has become the single 
prudential supervisor for large banks, capital rules are more consistent, while resolution 
planning is now much clearer than it was even a decade ago. But as the Draghi report 
noted, the incomplete Banking Union means Europe’s banks remain less profitable than 
their US counterparts.

### Page 17

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

There is a shift underway, though. The EU Commission plans to table a broad Banking 
Package in the first quarter of 2027, with measures covering cross-border capital and 
liquidity management, deposit insurance, prudential rules and other items. For M&A, the 
most important proposals include those to give banking groups more flexibility to 
allocate capital and liquidity more efficiently within cross-border groups, while also 
applying more consistent treatment to intra-group exposures. These remain proposals 
rather than agreed implementations, meaning their final scope and timetable remain 
uncertain. 
The AFME estimates that regulatory ring-fencing rules mean that some €225bn of 
capital and €250bn of liquidity is trapped within subsidiaries, which banks cannot freely 
move between parent and subsidiaries. Freeing this up could also support more cross-
border transactions. 
Regulatory ring-fencing traps subsidiaries' capital and liquidity

Source: ECB, ING

The European Commission is also bringing forward a new proposal to replace the 
European Deposit Insurance Scheme (EDIS), which could reduce national ring-fencing 
incentives and encourage more cross-border M&A. CMDI reform has been passed this 
year, which could make it easier to deal with failing banks, potentially making 
consolidation easier as well, although the direct impact on M&A is unclear. 
The Danish compromise, which is now embedded into CRR3, allows banks to risk-weight 
certain insurance subsidiaries rather than deduct them from regulatory capital. This was 
clarified by the EBA in January 2026, and some banks such as UniCredit have received 
authorisation to use the Danish Compromise methodology for its insurance subsidiaries. 
This has improved the economics of bancassurance and could support further bank-
insurance transactions. 
On the other hand, national regulatory and political intervention has also put the brakes 
on M&A in some cases, raising execution costs and uncertainty. BBVA’s proposed 
acquisition of Sabadell in 2024 illustrates the problem. It took 539 days from the 
announcement in May 2024 to the offer failing in October 2025. 
For us, the message seems clear that European regulators are seeking to improve bank 
competitiveness, and they see consolidation as an important part of this. Looking 
ahead, regulation can improve the plumbing, but it is not a dealmaker. 
Why 2027 should stay busy 
The starting point for 2027 remains supportive, with bank profitability continuing to hold 
up well in 2026 while capital levels remain solid. While the geopolitical, inflation and 
interest rate backdrop poses headwinds, we still expect acquisition activity to remain 
high. On the other hand, management teams will increasingly need to choose between 
€225bn
€250bn
Capital Liquidity

### Page 18

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

retaining greater capital headroom in a less market-friendly environment, shareholder 
payouts, or M&A. 
The most likely deals are still domestic. Italy remains the M&A market of choice, but 
other geographies could also stand out. The Nordics are likely to see more partnerships 
and tech- and wealth-focused deals. Spain and Portugal are already quite concentrated, 
so we could see more bolt-ons. In the UK, challenger banks and neobanks are gaining 
ground while deposit competition remains intense, potentially leading to more activity. 
The Benelux has potential catalysts following a series of government ownership 
reductions and increasing inorganic ambitions. 
Cross-border activity could also remain elevated, but we think the real litmus test 
remains the UniCredit-Commerzbank deal, including what happens after completion in 
terms of execution and integration, a process that is likely to take a few years. The 
regulatory environment should also become increasingly more supportive for cross-
border M&A from 2027 onwards. 
Overall, 2027 could be another interesting year. 
M&A is here to stay 
European bank M&A has picked up in recent years, and we expect more activity again in 
2027, although we do not see a big bang. For investors, positioning is inherently 
speculative. For bondholders, the main way to gain is by owning the target entity's 
bonds. However, this is not always the case, and bigger doesn’t always mean better, as 
credit ratings don’t necessarily improve either following an acquisition or merger. 
We are not suggesting that a major change in the European banking landscape is 
imminent, as many obstacles remain, particularly with cross-border deals. But the 
direction of travel is interesting: larger banks often outperform smaller banks, while 
regulators continue to see benefits from a deeper, more integrated European banking 
union. Still, European banking markets remain fragmented to differing degrees, both 
between countries and compared to the US, presenting both opportunities and 
obstacles.

### Page 19

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Bank capital enters 2027 on solid ground, while tight spreads and rising risks favour selectivity

AT1 reform remains on the back burner 
The European Commission published its report on competitiveness in the banking sector 
in July but did not include much in the way of proposals for AT1 bank capital 
instruments. The report mainly focused on reducing complexity, reporting requirements 
and improving competitiveness for Europe’s banking sector. 
For AT1, this probably means that the instruments are unlikely to be phased out 
anytime soon. What is somewhat relevant for AT1s is the Commission’s intention to 
reduce complexity for bank capital structures. This includes making the Pillar 2 
requirement and guidance more transparent, while the Commission also proposes to 
consolidate parts of the buffers into two buffers: one that can be released in periods of 
stress, and another that would be maintained in all scenarios. 
This, in theory, could reduce the risk of coupon skips, as being able to release buffers 
during periods of stress would not automatically lead to a breach of the combined buffer 
requirements. The proposals also include a removal of the leverage ratio Pillar 2 capital 
requirement, which would affect the largest European systemically important banks 
subject to this requirement. This is only a small number of banks but could (in theory) 
lead to a marginal decline in AT1 issuance. 
Overall, AT1 reform remains part of a broader discussion, with the EU Commission 
proposals also likely to change as negotiations remain ongoing and consensus needs to 
be built. For AT1, we expect the instruments to keep playing an important role (yay!) in 
European banks’ capital stacks, with no signs of an imminent abolition or grandfathering 
process. 
More supply while refinancing remains the main event 
With no major changes to AT1 structures or requirements imminent, we don’t expect a 
significant impact on banks’ capital instrument management and funding plans in 2027.

Jesse Norcross 
Senior Sector Strategist, Real Estate 
jesse.norcross@ing.com

Why AT1 still matters in 2027  
AT1 reform may remain on the back burner in 2027, leaving the instruments' role in bank 
capital largely unchanged. Solid fundamentals remain supportive, while supply should be 
manageable – but tight(ish) spreads and low reset levels could weigh on the market

### Page 20

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Primary issuance in euro-denominated AT1 and Tier 2 has been in line with expectations 
in 2026. EUR AT1 issuance has just passed €15bn at the time of writing, slightly above 
our expectation of €15bn. Similarly, EUR Tier 2 has reached €27bn, nearing our 
expectation of €30bn for 2026. 
In 2027, we expect a modest increase in supply, largely dominated by the refinancing of 
upcoming redemptions/calls. We expect €20bn in AT1 supply, compared to €14bn of 
calls. While this represents a step-up, incremental net supply is only around €3bn higher 
than in 2026. Tier 2 redemptions will also rise by around €3bn next year, supporting our 
increased supply expectation of €35bn in Tier 2. 
While gross supply will be higher, net supply should remain easily absorbable. More 
supply should create opportunities in primary markets, although lower reset levels have 
left some recently issued bonds more exposed during weaker markets. 
AT1 redemptions pick up next year (€bn)

Source: IGM, ING

French banks: Déjà vu? 
French sovereign risk has increased, with the 10-year OAT-Bund spread now trading at 
around 160bp at the time of writing, the highest level since 2011. The French 10y yield is 
around 5%, the highest level since 2002. The story in French markets is growing more 
sinister by the day. But contagion risk across the eurozone is (so far) relatively 
contained. Italy and Belgium stand out as other higher-risk countries, given high debt 
levels and deficits, but it remains a French story for now. Nonetheless, the domestic 
sovereign exposure and the sovereign-bank nexus have come back into play. 
So far, the market is not questioning the broader future of the eurozone. Still, France is of 
a much larger magnitude than countries such as Greece or even Italy, which had to be 
supported during the eurozone crisis. 
What can be said is that French banks, as well as the broader European banking sector, 
are in much better shape than they were during the eurozone crisis. French banks’ 
capital ratios have increased significantly and now stand at around 13-15% on average, 
compared to c.9-10% during the eurozone crisis. Liquidity levels are healthy, while 
earnings and exposures, particularly for the larger banks, are well diversified. For 
instance, BNP Paribas generated around 25% of its revenues in 2025 in France, while 
French deposits account for 29% of the total. For Société Générale, France accounts for 
40% of exposure. 
French banks’ direct exposure to OATs is also not that large; it represents around 6% of 
total assets, while roughly two-thirds is held at amortised cost, making holdings less 
sensitive to valuation declines. Some French banks do carry higher exposure, particularly 
those with limited geographic diversification. For instance, La Banque Postale shows the

### Page 21

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

highest OAT exposure as a percentage of CET1, at 114%. But around 95% is held at 
amortised cost. 
On average, for most French banks, OAT holdings represent around 15-30% of CET1. For 
context, the data suggests that a 100bp widening of the large French banks’ domestic 
sovereign holdings would lower CET1 for BNP and Société Générale by less than 10bp, 
and up to 25bp for Crédit Agricole. 
French banks OAT exposure appears manageable

Source: Company reports, EBA, ING

Still, the risks are rising and sustained high OAT-Bund levels could feed into the real 
economy, credit demand, confidence and asset quality. While non-performing loans 
(NPLs) are low for French banks, they have risen a bit in recent quarters, and we could 
see more procyclical provisioning from French banks, in particular for SMEs and 
consumer loans. 
Risks could also arise from other avenues. Moody’s surprisingly maintained France’s 
rating at Aa3, with a negative outlook, despite broad expectations of a downgrade. 
Nonetheless, rating downgrades remain a risk, and S&P is due for review at the end of 
November, bringing further sovereign ratings pressure. 
A larger move in OATs would also be more disruptive, as higher yields, social unrest and 
political fragmentation complicate deficit reduction. October’s preliminary composite 
PMI of 48.4 adds to the weaker-growth picture. 
However, we do not think this is the same sovereign-bank loop as in 2009-2012. 
European Central Bank funding facilities, the Bank Recovery and Resolution Directive 
(BRRD) and stronger liquidity coverage ratio (LCR) and net stable funding ratio (NSFR) 
requirements have reduced banks’ dependence on sovereign guarantees. The link 
remains: ratings, domestic bond holdings and a shared operating environment still 
transmit sovereign stress into bank funding costs and market access. 
The flip side is that liquidity requirements and favourable EU sovereign risk weights have 
made banks important buyers of government debt. As ECB purchases ended and TLTROs 
were repaid, sovereign holdings have partly replaced central bank reserves for LCR 
purposes. Banks rely less on sovereign guarantees, but on the other hand, sovereigns 
arguably rely more on banks for funding. 
This new relationship, alongside expectations that the ECB would contain broader 
spillovers, perhaps helps explain why bank spreads have not widened as much as OATs. 
The ECB’s refinancing and bond purchase tools remain part of the toolkit, although they 
have previously been used in a different inflation and rates environment.

Without sovereign relief, pressure is likely to persist, particularly for domestically 
focused banks with larger relative OAT holdings. Limited issuance may slow the 
repricing, but the distinction between issuers remains important, especially in AT1. 
French AT1 underperforms 
French AT1 has underperformed YTD and now trades wider than most other major 
country cohorts in EUR AT1. That mostly reflects heightened sovereign-bank nexus 
concerns and OAT-Bund spread widening, rather than a deterioration in fundamentals. 
However, it also reflects market concerns about potential ratings impacts and ongoing 
political and fiscal volatility, as well as a weakening in the macro environment. 
We would avoid treating all French banks similarly. Sensitivity to OATs and the French 
economy differ quite a bit across issuers. As such, the recent widening has been quite 
uniform, but the market could become more selective in French AT1s if nervousness 
continues into 2027. We think the most diversified names, including BNP and ACAFP in 
2027 and 2028 callable bonds, with high reset levels, should hold up better. 
French AT1 spreads trade at the widest levels in EUR

Source: IHS Markit, ING

Calling remains the base case, while the economics are changing 
Banks have continued to call their AT1s this year, and calls (or tenders) remain our base 
case for 2027. While the decision to call or extend is highly dependent on market 
circumstances and, to a lesser extent, driven by regulatory approval, we still see a high 
bar for not calling/replacing. 
AT1 reset levels have hit lows in 2026

Source: IHS Markit, ING

This is especially the case as the reset levels remain low, while overall coupon levels are 
in line with recent historical averages. The economics become less comfortable further

AT1 and Tier 2: Spread widening at different speeds 
The EUR AT1 index has widened by 19bp year-to-date, outperforming callable and bullet 
Tier 2. Over the last three months, the picture looks different, though. AT1 has 
underperformed Tier 2 as recent market stress has led to quite a bit of widening. On a 
beta-adjusted basis, AT1 has had a mixed performance over three months but has 
outperformed on a YTD basis, suggesting the recent sell-off has reversed the trend a 
little bit 
AT1 has widened in recent months as volatility has come back to markets

Source: IHS Markit, ING

However, the correction has only moved spreads from tight to merely quite tight. The 
AT1 index now trades at a similar level to the 2y median of asw+295bp. Callable Tier 2 
also trades just inside the 2y median after the recent widening. The AT1-Callable Tier 2 
differential is around asw+150bp, while the AT1/Callable Tier 2 ratio is also in line with 
the 2-year average. 
Both remain quite compressed compared to historical levels, suggesting that on spread 
alone, Tier 2 currently offers better value. We see somewhat greater widening risk in 
2027, as bank fundamentals soften at the margin, while supply rises a bit and entry 
valuations remain slightly compressed. 
This does not make AT1 too rich, but the yield angle remains the key driver of why AT1 
could continue to perform well. With rates likely to remain elevated, additional yield 
combined with some spread widening may lead investors to wait for better entry points.

### Page 24

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Reset has mattered more than ratings 
High reset spread AT1s have generally been more resilient in 2026, with the 700-800bp 
reset bucket even bucking the trend with some tightening over the last three months. 
While this relationship is not fully linear, and many high-reset bonds are issued by 
higher beta/lower rated issuers, numbers still suggest that reset levels have been an 
important driver of performance this year. Higher underlying rates reduce the cushion 
that lower reset AT1 bonds have had, as extension risk concerns have made something 
of a comeback in recent months. 
Higher reset levels also provide greater coupon protection if a bond is extended during a 
period of market stress. There are a few near-term low-reset bonds callable with reset 
levels below +380bp. However, extension risk is still low for these: longer-call AT1s from 
these issuers trade through the respective reset levels, and these banks have historically 
assessed calls holistically, rather than purely on an economics or bond-by-bond basis. 
Higher-rated bonds have underperformed in 2026. IG-rated bonds have not translated 
into better spread performance: BBB- rated AT1s now trade at similar spread levels as 
BB+ and BB-rated AT1s. 
Reset levels have been a better guide for performance than credit ratings in 2026 
High reset AT1s have been more resilient...  … while higher rated bonds have seen less love

Source: IHS Markit, ING  Source: IHS Markit, ING

So where does that leave the AT1 market in 2027? 
2027 is shaping up to be another interesting year for bank capital. Valuations look a 
bit compressed, but bank fundamentals remain solid, with banks likely to continue 
calling their instruments at the first call dates. In terms of supply, we expect the 
small increase to be absorbable but potentially lead to investors becoming a bit 
more price sensitive. Furthermore, the lack of imminent AT1 reform should mean 
the asset class retains an important role in Europe’s banking capital stacks for the 
foreseeable future. 
There are risks becoming more visible, not least through sovereign and fiscal 
concerns, with France the obvious victim at the moment. A softening of 
fundamentals at the margin and lower reset levels further along the call curve could 
create more volatility. Spreads have recently seen some widening, yet there is 
probably greater scope for spreads to stay wider than for further tightening in 2027. 
AT1 yields could remain a good driver of demand for investors, cushioning potential 
spread weakness. On the other hand, the market is likely to differentiate more 
between structures and issuer profiles, while remaining wary of banks with greater 
domestic sovereign sensitivity.
